STIL (full available entry and applicable directory notes):

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to

>          Thankfully I had a good instrument set to start with, and
>          worked from there to get something that sounded fast and gradually
>          build up a bit. It was all done in a day or so." (WP)

> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>          "Zaw Productions" was my own C64 music group which I formed on my 19th
>          birthday, 10th May 1991. I kept it going and made various C64 tunes
>          and music demos until December 1999 when I was asked if I'd like to
>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> COMMENT: "This was my entry for the Transmission64 music competition. I wanted
>          to do something different, in terms of using a flute instrument as
>          the main lead, and build around that, with verse, chorus and middle
>          sections to set it all off. It did work as I intended, and there's
>          also a hidden meaning to the title - the initials of the title spell
>          out TULL, a nod to Jethro Tull and particularly Ian Anderson, who is
>          the vocalist and flute player." (WP)

> COMMENT: "This was made for the hidden part of the Cheetahmen demo. One of the
>          group (Tas) is from Yorkshire, and he likes the music from Falcon
>          Patrol II quite a bit, and was interested in getting some C64 remixers
>          remixing the tune. I decided it'd be nice to do my own tribute to the
>          game theme by keeping the spirit of the original intact, but with some
>          different instruments. The title "What's wrong wi' it?" is a phrase
>          used by Tas, which is Yorkshire shorthand for "What's wrong with it?"
>          and thought it'd be a nice idea to name it that." (WP)

>
> COMMENT: "This was a work in progress of the title tune for the Arsenal FC
>          game licence which never was released. I wanted to use a football
>          type handclap chant as the main drum, and then throughout the title
>          tune it was going to reference some football songs with the drum
>          backing. The disk was thought to be lost when I moved house many
>          years ago, but found an unlabelled disk, and saw the file names on
>          there. When I loaded them into Ubik's Musik it was indeed this,
>          so pretty good to salvage it all round.
>          On the same disk as the Arsenal title tune, I'd put together a few
>          sound effects which could have played when you were playing the game
>          of football. I was also considering some football chants such as
>          those I'd used in the Football Tunes demo as well." (WP)

>          though it's intentionally wrong that way in the game. So many people
>          remember how bad that music was, calling it not a tune, so feel free
>          to sing along not a tune to this too!" (WP)

>          join Padua. I was more than happy to, so I ceased Zaw Productions at
>          the same time." (WP)
>
> Directory note /MUSICIANS/W/Waz/Worktunes/
> COMMENT: All Warren's comments are indicated by (WP).
>
> COMMENT: "This was the first known version of the demo. It was Action Replay
>          frozen and sent off to Zzap! 64 for evaluation for their cover
>          mount cassette. I had feed back from them a while later (they had
>          reformed as Commodore Force due to the financial collapse of the
>          publisher Newsfield) to ask if I could change the voices used on
>          the tunes, as they said it sounded the same. In a way, it was a
>          good thing because I went back to the demo and spent some time
>          cleaning the music up and making it sound a whole lot nicer in
>          the end." (WP)
> (#1)
>    NAME: The Wrong Side of Bed
> (#2)
>    NAME: Aural Ambience Attack
> (#3)
>    NAME: Angelic Hell for Halves
> (#4)
>    NAME: Gun Runner
> (#5)
>    NAME: Into the Grey Matter
> (#6)
>    NAME: Hope in a Nuclear World

>          12" single and played that a fair bit too." (WP)
